=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Takahashi aggregated usage records from ticket gates.
However, he accidentally erased some records of entering and exiting stations.
He is trying to restore the erased records.

You are given a string S consisting of i and o. We want to insert zero or more characters at arbitrary positions in S so that the resulting string satisfies the following conditions:

- Its length is even, and every odd-numbered (1st, 3rd, ...) character is i while every even-numbered (2nd, 4th, ...) character is o.

Find the minimum number of characters that need to be inserted. It can be proved under the constraints of this problem that by inserting an appropriate finite number of characters, S can be made to satisfy the conditions.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
S

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- S is a string of length between 1 and 100, consisting of i and o.

Sample Input 1

ioi

Sample Output 1

1

We can insert o after the 3rd character to form ioio to satisfy the conditions. The conditions cannot be satisfied by inserting zero or fewer characters.

Sample Input 2

iioo

Sample Output 2

2

We can insert o after the 1st character and i after the 3rd character to satisfy the conditions. The conditions cannot be satisfied by inserting one or fewer characters.

Sample Input 3

io

Sample Output 3

0

S already satisfies the conditions.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys
S=sys.stdin.readline().strip()
ans=0
need='i'
for c in S:
    if c != need:
        ans += 1
    need = 'o' if c == 'i' else 'i'
if (len(S)+ans)%2 == 1:
    ans += 1
print(ans)

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:
import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    ans = 0
    need = 0  # 0 means 'i', 1 means 'o'
    for ch in S:
        cur = 0 if ch == 'i' else 1
        if cur != need:
            ans += 1
        need = 1 - cur
    if (len(S) + ans) % 2 == 1:
        ans += 1
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    ans = 0
    need = 'i'
    for ch in S:
        if ch != need:
            ans += 1
        need = 'o' if ch == 'i' else 'i'
    if (len(S) + ans) % 2 == 1:
        ans += 1
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```
import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    ans = 0
    need = 'i'
    for ch in S:
        if ch != need:
            ans += 1
        need = 'o' if ch == 'i' else 'i'
    if (len(S) + ans) % 2:
        ans += 1
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Which code should I final? I think need loop is more universally understandable and directly implements the insertion process. It is also safe
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    ans = 0
    need = 'i'

    for ch in S:
        if ch != need:
            ans += 1
        need = 'o' if ch == 'i' else 'i'

    if (len(S) + ans) % 2 == 1:
        ans += 1

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
